# COLL006.C1.P4 — Bare-Bracket Migration Census

**T8 baseline:** COMPLETE; 496 in-scope AST literal nodes and 0 `AMBIGUOUS` or unclassified entries before T9 source migration. **G5 inventory:** 495 classified bare bracket nodes; its retained rows already include the T10 cutover fixtures. **T12 delta refresh:** 505 classified bare bracket nodes after adding the T11 fixtures, with 0 `AMBIGUOUS` or unclassified entries. The ten new *bare* nodes are nine semantic incremental literals and one explicitly contextual List trace fixture. Explicitly headed Array/Vector literals are excluded by the original walker rule.

## Method and coverage

- The AST walker traversed **1,709 `.ph` files** and recorded **342** bare `Expr::BracketLiteral` nodes. It distinguishes explicit-head literals and subscript expressions and records nested literals separately. Parser recovery trees are included in discovery. The scan encountered parser diagnostics in **311 files / 8,593 diagnostics**; these counts describe repository coverage, not a clean parse result.

- A Rust string scan inspected **44,293** ordinary/raw string literals and found **332** parser-produced bracket nodes across candidate strings. After excluding selector names, expected source slices, printed-output expectations, parse-recovery fragments, TOML/configuration metadata, and implementation-format strings, **153** embedded Phalcom test-source nodes remain in scope, plus one parser unit-test input at `phalcom-ast/src/parser.rs`.

- The Rust scan observed **17,729** fragments with parser diagnostics. Recovered AST-shaped hits in those fragments were reviewed and excluded where they came from TOML metadata, selector syntax, tags, or malformed format text. Rust strings are included only when they are actual Phalcom source consumed by a test/helper.

- The temporary helper self-check passed for nested bare literals, explicit-head distinction, subscripts, raw/ordinary Rust strings, and lifetime/character-literal scanning.

- The accepted decisions and current spec index were reviewed separately because their prose and inline examples are not repository source fixtures. T9 updated both accepted decisions, added the current sequence-literal spec and index entry, and clarified that the VM List builders require a checked List family.

`.ph` paths are repository-relative. Rust rows give the Rust file/line containing the source string, then the embedded Phalcom line and byte range. The local-evidence cell shows the AST node in its immediate source context. Each row records its classification reason, required migration, and owning test/example/API impact.

## Classification keys

| Class | Classification rule | Required migration |
|---|---|---|
| `LIST_RESIZING` | The value must support List size changes such as append, prepend, insert, remove, pop, clear, or removeAll. | Give the producer an explicit `List<T>` context or construct a List at the source boundary; keep the resizing operation and its test. |
| `LIST_IDENTITY` | A declared List type, List-only API, or legacy collection-identity assertion requires a nominal List value at this producer. | Retain an existing `List<T>` expected type/signature or add one at the producer/call boundary; do not depend on the bare default. |
| `FIXED_RUNTIME` | The site has Array/Vector context or intentionally exercises the fixed-size runtime Array/Vector family. | Keep explicit Array/Vector context where the family is asserted; leave an uncontextual runtime fixture bare when it intentionally targets future Array. |
| `STATIC_IMMUTABLE` | The literal is a static constant with no explicit List family requirement; static evaluation publishes an immutable sequence. | Leave uncontextual constants bare when they exercise the future static Vector default; retain an explicit `Vector<T, N>` context when the fixture asserts a family or extent. |
| `READ_ITERATE_ONLY` | This runtime source reads, iterates, searches, renders, or consumes the sequence without a List-only identity or size-change requirement; its target family is Array. | Leave the literal bare so it selects runtime Array after T10. If an API needs List identity, classify the producer as `LIST_IDENTITY` and give it explicit List context. |
| `LEGACY_TEST` | This site preserves a legacy List-family oracle or tests bracket grammar without depending on collection family. | If retaining List behavior, state `List<T>` explicitly; otherwise update the expected family/result to Array or Vector. Grammar-only tests retain their syntax assertion. |

## T12 refresh inventory

The G5 table above has 495 site rows (confirmed by counting rows with source
locations). It already contains the two T10 cutover test rows at
`coll006_c1_p4_cutover.rs`, as well as the bare static default fixture in
`static_constants.rs`. The following *new* bare source nodes occur in T11
Rust-embedded Phalcom fixtures. Each source string is a separate test input,
including the repeated cold-analysis control; source line numbers identify
the containing Rust string at this revision. Their intended family and
classification have been reviewed individually:

| Source fixture and embedded literal | Class | Evidence / impact |
|---|---|---|
| `phalcom-semantic/tests/semantic/incremental/coll006_c1_p3_publication.rs:151` `const values = [1, 2, 3]` | `STATIC_IMMUTABLE` | Removing an Array annotation selects static Vector; no List behavior needed. |
| `phalcom-semantic/tests/semantic/incremental/coll006_c1_p3_publication.rs:154` `let values = [1, 2, 3]` | `FIXED_RUNTIME` | Runtime phase selects Array. |
| `phalcom-semantic/tests/semantic/incremental/coll006_c1_p3_publication.rs:157` `const values = [1, 2, 3, 4]` | `STATIC_IMMUTABLE` | Static Vector extent changes from three to four. |
| `phalcom-semantic/tests/semantic/incremental/coll006_c1_p3_publication.rs:267` `const values = []` | `STATIC_IMMUTABLE` | Empty static Vector is intentionally underconstrained without `T`; no value is published. |
| `phalcom-semantic/tests/semantic/incremental/coll006_c1_p3_publication.rs:277` `const values = [1, 2]` | `STATIC_IMMUTABLE` | Valid static Vector recovery after underconstraint. |
| `phalcom-semantic/tests/semantic/incremental/coll006_c1_p3_publication.rs:281` `const values: Vector<Int, 2> = [1]` | `STATIC_IMMUTABLE` | Contextual static Vector with invalid extent fails closed. |
| `phalcom-semantic/tests/semantic/incremental/coll006_c1_p3_publication.rs:293` `const values: Vector<Int, 2> = [*source]` | `STATIC_IMMUTABLE` | Exact static Vector spread retains its checked proof. |
| `phalcom-semantic/tests/semantic/incremental/coll006_c1_p3_publication.rs:302` `const values: Vector<Int, 2> = [*source]` | `STATIC_IMMUTABLE` | Runtime-sized source loses the Vector proof; no stale static value. |

T12 delta: `LIST_RESIZING` +1, `FIXED_RUNTIME` +1,
`STATIC_IMMUTABLE` +8; all other classes unchanged. Thus **505** sites
(`495 + 10`) are classified, with no new List migration needed. Lines for
these fixtures may move with later formatting; the embedded source snippets
and classification are the stable evidence.

| `docs/decisions/accepted/0028-collections-representation-and-literals.md` | TDR-0028 now points bracket family selection to TDR-0091 and no longer describes all brackets as List desugaring. | Complete; the amended umbrella is aligned with contextual, phase-sensitive semantics. |

## T9 takeover

- T9 migrated each `LIST_RESIZING` and `LIST_IDENTITY` producer to explicit List context, a List parameter/result contract, or a typed intermediate. The row evidence shows the current context at each AST node.
- Runtime-only read/iteration sites remain bare only where they intentionally exercise the future Array default; the outgoing-pack and view-to-List fixtures use generic iterable sources and do not require List identity.
- List-specific examples and fixtures retain their behavior through explicit List intent. Parser/highlighter-only fixtures remain syntax-only.
- T3 decision carried into this census: symbolic Vector identity spread remains unreduced during static evaluation, keeping sequence values closed.
- G5 refresh found no ambiguous or unclassified source nodes. Focused semantic and core Array/Vector gates are recorded in the checkpoint after they pass.
